# CRP Global Legal-Source Catalogue

**Status:** Approved Corpus Contract — Rank 4  
**Catalogue version:** `CRP-GLOBAL-LEGAL-SOURCE-1`  
**Effective date:** 2026-09-29

This catalogue preserves the complete certified legal-content baseline admitted through the vetted,
digest-bound legacy legal-corpus boundary. It is authoritative for the recorded legal-source content,
citations, source metadata, effective information, jurisdiction references, legal-family assignments,
and recorded gaps of its 437 entries. It is not executable authority and is not itself a governed
legal-rule corpus.

`ADMITTED_SOURCE_ONLY` remains the historical capture label stored in each entry. Under the certified
baseline policy, that label means the entry is an authoritative baseline source record, not merely an
unverified research lead.

No entry in this catalogue may produce a `VIOLATION` or `PROBABLE_VIOLATION` until a later owner
amendment re-expresses it as a complete governed legal rule under
`CRP_GOVERNED_LEGAL_CORPUS_CONTRACT.md`.

## 1. Catalogue Scope

```text
TOTAL SOURCE ENTRIES: 437
CONTENT_RULE: 255
LIMITATION_RECORD: 111
AUTHORITY_RECORD: 27
CITATION_RECORD: 23
GAP: 18
REFUSAL: 3
ADMITTED_SOURCE_ONLY: 437
GOVERNED LEGAL COVERAGE: 0
PERMITTED LEGAL FINDINGS: 0
```

The catalogue uses `CRP-LEGACY-JURISDICTION-NORMALIZATION-1` for source-token normalization and
`CRP-JURISDICTION-ENUM-1` as the sole canonical jurisdiction vocabulary.

## 2. Entry Schema

Every entry in §4 must contain exactly these fields:

```text
source_entry_id
record_type
legal_family
legacy_jurisdiction_reference
canonical_jurisdiction_status
canonical_country_code
canonical_region_code
instrument_title
provision_or_citation
source_locator
effective_information_as_recorded
verification_state_as_recorded
source_artifact
source_artifact_sha256
catalogue_status
```

Rules:

- `source_entry_id` is immutable, unique, and ordered `CRP-LSRC-0001` through `CRP-LSRC-0437`.
- `canonical_country_code` and `canonical_region_code` are JSON `null` for country-wide sources,
  gaps, or refusals without an exact region mapping.
- Preserve every recorded instrument title, provision/citation, locator, date/effective field, and
  verification field exactly as reported in PHASE2-001J.
- Use `NOT RECORDED` only where the admitted source truly lacks a value.
- Use `WITHHELD_EXCLUDED_TEXT` only where the sole value is excluded statute-body or engine-linked text.
- `catalogue_status` is exactly `ADMITTED_SOURCE_ONLY` for every entry.
- No field may contain statute body text, evaluator trigger, report anchor, engine class, score,
  confidence value, finding outcome, or implementation reference.

## 3. Canonical-Jurisdiction Notes

Country-wide sources remain source descriptors and have no region assignment. They require a later
explicit governed-rule relation to an exact selected region.

`US-UM` remains the sole canonical jurisdiction with `NO_LEGACY_SOURCE_ENTRY`. No source entry may
invent coverage for it.

The four legacy `UK-*` source forms use the explicit source-only mappings to `GB-ENG`, `GB-NIR`,
`GB-SCT`, and `GB-WLS`. Those mappings are not consumer-input aliases.

## 4. Global Source Entries

Include exactly 437 JSON objects in one JSON array, ordered exactly as the PHASE2-001J manifest:

```text
1. United States federal
2. United States states and territories
3. Canada federal
4. Canada provinces and territories
5. United Kingdom nations
6. Australia states and territories
```

    "instrument_title": "state/territory limitation statutes (varies by debt type and by state) — Recorded `SOL_GAPS` entry for `US-*` (read per unit; the wildcard token identifies no single unit) — PHASE5-001C owner amendment (wildcard preserved; this gap is NOT closed; see CRP_GAP_REFUSAL_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md): the owner document supplies an ingestion and session-metadata design that would resolve US-* from the consumer session or account profile, with a fallback to session state where the wildcard persists.     "instrument_title": "`provincial and territorial Acts` (per the entry's own naming) — Every province and territory is in the table with an instrument; the recorded gap concerns how those instruments relate to the federal baseline — PHASE5-001C owner amendment (relationship recorded; cross-layer conflict remains open; see CRP_GAP_REFUSAL_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md): the owner document records the federal baseline as PIPEDA for privacy with Bank Act and FCAC guidelines for banking, and records that every province and territory already has an instrument in the table. Owner-supplied relationship statements: (i) for a federal bank the federal baseline takes priority while provincial data-accuracy and privacy laws are still respected absent direct conflict; (ii) for a provincial lender or collector the provincial instrument governs operational timelines such as credit aging and collections; (iii) for a conflict in timelines the owner document proposes applying the shortest, most consumer-friendly window. Statement (iii) is recorded as a proposal only and is NOT adopted: this amendment applies no shortest-period and no global override rule, so cross-layer conflict resolution remains unresolved. Provincial examples named by the owner document are Ontario (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.O. 1990) and Quebec (Civil Code of Québec / Consumer Protection Act), together with British Columbia and Alberta in the same supplied matrix. Province-specific rules are preserved as supplied and no merged, superseding or overridden rule is created",
    "provision_or_citation": "NOT RECORDED",
    "source_locator": "NOT RECORDED",
    "effective_information_as_recorded": "NOT RECORDED",
    "verification_state_as_recorded": "Owner-authorised relationship statements recorded under PHASE5-001C; owner document SHA-256 8FA1D3D92DF2166FA515B2B54E703D2772CAC225C132A32E8677A7E5BA16FC62. The instrument relationships are recorded as supplied and were not independently retrieved; the proposed shortest-period conflict rule is not adopted and stays unresolved; no province-specific rule is merged, overridden or applied by this amendment and no rule is entered",
    "source_artifact": "packages\\backend\\src\\services\\legalRules.ts",
    "source_artifact_sha256": "FE5C1BA63AE85923E378D4278C3D6E85461E8DF648847F43FE02FB4185BD41F6",

    "instrument_title": "`FCAC` published bureau practice — The recorded FCAC periods are the bureaus' published practice; no statutory basis is cited for each period — PHASE5-001C owner amendment (statutory-basis identifications supplied for the named provinces; retrieval still owed; see CRP_GAP_REFUSAL_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md): the owner document supplies the following owner-supplied statutory bases for the recorded bureau reporting periods, each given as bankruptcy-first years / collections-and-judgments years: Ontario 7/6 (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.O. 1990, c. C.33, s. 9(3)); Quebec 6/6 (Civil Code of Québec, CQLR c CCQ-1991, art. 30); British Columbia 6/6 (Business Practices and Consumer Protection Act, S.B.C. 2004, c. 2, s. 109); Alberta 6/6 (Consumer Protection Act, R.S.A. 2000, c. C-26.3 with the Consumer Reporting Regulation, s. 16); Manitoba 6/6 (Personal Investigations Act, C.C.S.M. c. P34, s. 9(3)); Saskatchewan 6/6 (The Credit Reporting Act, S.S. 2004, c. C-43.2, s. 18); Nova Scotia 6/6 (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.N.S. 1989, c. 89, s. 11(3)); New Brunswick 7/6 (Consumer Reporting Act, S.N.B. 2014, c. 31, s. 13); Prince Edward Island 7/6 (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.P.E.I. 1988, c. C-18, s. 10); Newfoundland and Labrador 7/6 (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.N.L. 1990, c. C-32, s. 11); Yukon, Northwest Territories and Nunavut 6/6 respectively under the Consumers Protection Act or Consumer Protection Act, with no precise provision supplied. None of these provisions was retrieved in this work order, so no period is admitted as a verified statutory basis and no rule is entered. Provenance limitation: the owner document cites the Prince Edward Island Consumer Reporting Act as c. C-18 in this row and as c. C-20 in the CRP-LSRC-0323 row",

C-18 here, c. C-20 in CRP-LSRC-0323) and remains unresolved",

  {

    "instrument_title": "the four provincial consumer-reporting regimes (named by the entry) — Those regimes are read in their own words and deliberately not entered; the entry records the blocking reason — PHASE5-001C owner amendment (exclusion provisions recorded; the deliberate non-entry is retained; see CRP_GAP_REFUSAL_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md): the owner document supplies the provisions for the four regimes read and deliberately not entered — Ontario (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.O. 1990, c. C.33, s. 2(2) and s. 2(3): carve-outs where the Act does not apply, including professional investigations and specialised financial data transfers); Nova Scotia (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.N.S. 1989, c. 89, s. 2: scope limited to consumer reports compiled for profit or commercial dissemination); New Brunswick (Consumer Reporting Act, S.N.B. 2014, c. 31, s. 2: limits on what constitutes an active consumer reporting file); Prince Edward Island (Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.P.E.I. 1988, c. C-20, s. 2: jurisdictional parameters and exemptions for commercial credit lines or cross-border file processing). The deliberate non-entry of these regimes is retained and is now grounded as an owner-supplied scope decision; the provisions were not retrieved in this work order, so no rule is entered and no exclusion is applied by this amendment. Provenance limitation: the Prince Edward Island chapter number is given as c. C-20 here and as c. C-18 in the CRP-LSRC-0322 row",

    "instrument_title": "(bureau-side duties; named by the entry) — Bureau-side duties read and deliberately not entered; the decision is recorded as made — PHASE5-001C owner amendment (bureau-side duties recorded; scoping retained and out of scope for report-only findings; see CRP_GAP_REFUSAL_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md): the owner document supplies the bureau-side statutory frameworks that were read and deliberately not entered. Canada: Ontario Consumer Reporting Act, R.S.O. 1990, c. C.33, s. 8 (duty to maintain accurate files) and s. 13 (30-day dispute investigation framework); Civil Code of Québec, CQLR c CCQ-1991, art. 35 to 40 (reputation and privacy protections) with the Act respecting the protection of personal information in the private sector; British Columbia Business Practices and Consumer Protection Act, S.B.C. 2004, c. 2, Part 6, s. 107 to 112 (consumer reporting agency responsibilities and mandatory file corrections). United Kingdom: UK Data Protection Act 2018 and UK GDPR Article 5(1)(d) (accuracy principle); Consumer Credit Act 1974, s. 157 to 159 (disclosure of file contents and the correction and notice framework). The recorded reason for omission is that this node enforces data-furnisher obligations only. Bureau-side duties and bureau conduct cannot be established from report content, so they remain out of scope for report-only findings and are not entered as rules",
    "provision_or_citation": "NOT RECORDED",
    "source_locator": "NOT RECORDED",

    "instrument_title": "`Credit Reference Agency Information Notice (CRAIN)` — the searches row only — Recorded `UNVERIFIED_GAPS` entry: the SEARCHES row only remains unverified; the other three were closed by owner approval 2026-09-17 — PHASE5-001C owner amendment (search distinctions recorded as owner-authorised source information; the SEARCHES row is NOT verified and this gap is NOT closed; see CRP_GAP_REFUSAL_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md): the owner document supplies the CRAIN search distinctions — a hard search (credit application) is stated to be retained and visible to other lenders for twelve months from the date of the search; a soft search (quotation, eligibility, audit) is stated to be visible only to the consumer and the bureau, invisible to third-party lenders, decoupled from credit scoring and typically purged or archived after twelve to twenty-four months depending on the specific bureau system architecture, without affecting risk metrics. The owner document cites UK GDPR Article 5(1)(c) (data minimisation) and Article 5(1)(e) (storage limitation) as interpreted by the official CRAIN Section 5 framework. These statements are recorded as owner-supplied only: the twelve-month figure is a CRAIN framework retention statement and is NOT recorded as a specific statutory twelve-month rule, CRAIN is an industry notice rather than primary legislation and remains outside the primary-authority scope, and a search row cannot support a report-content finding",
    "provision_or_citation": "NOT RECORDED",
    "source_locator": "NOT RECORDED",
    "effective_information_as_recorded": "NOT RECORDED",
    "verification_state_as_recorded": "Owner-authorised CRAIN search statements recorded under PHASE5-001C; owner document SHA-256 8FA1D3D92DF2166FA515B2B54E703D2772CAC225C132A32E8677A7E5BA16FC62.   {

    "instrument_title": "state/territory Limitation Acts — Recorded `SOL_GAPS` entry: state/territory law; no Australian national period exists (the wildcard token identifies no single unit) — PHASE5-001C owner amendment (wildcard preserved; this gap is NOT closed; the supplied mapping is held separately in the register; see CRP_GAP_REFUSAL_RESOLUTION_REGISTER.md): the owner document supplies the following exact-jurisdiction limitation mapping, recorded separately from this unresolved wildcard — New South Wales 6 years (Limitation Act 1969 (NSW), s. 14(1)(a)); Victoria 6 years (Limitation of Actions Act 1958 (Vic), s. 5(1)(a)); Queensland 6 years (Limitation of Actions Act 1974 (Qld), s. 10(1)(a)); Western Australia 6 years (Limitation Act 2005 (WA), s. 13 and s. 84, with the debt recorded as extinguished); South Australia 6 years (Limitation of Actions Act 1936 (SA), s. 35); Tasmania 6 years (Limitation Act 1974 (Tas), s. 4(1)(a)); Northern Territory 3 years (Limitation Act 1981 (NT), s. 12(1)(a)); Australian Capital Territory 6 years (Limitation Act 1985 (ACT), s. 11(1)(a)).
